\title{The toral subgroup of $G(\mathfrak{m})$ commutes with the unipotent subgroup up to a unipotent factor: a formal verification}
\date{}
\begin{document}
\maketitle
\begin{abstract}
For the group $G(\mathfrak{m})$, defined by generators and relations attached to the Monster Lie algebra, let $H$ be the toral subgroup and $U$ the unipotent subgroup. Lemma 3.5 of the source states that for all $h\in H$ and $u\in U$ there is a $u'\in U$ with $uh=hu'$. We present a Lean 4/Mathlib formalization of this lemma, where the coefficients $c(j)$ of the modular function $J$ are replaced by an arbitrary function $c:\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}$. The proof uses the previously verified formal version of Theorem 3.4, that $H$ normalizes $U$, together with one elementary group identity.
\end{abstract}

\section{Statement}

Let $\C$ be the complex numbers and $\N$ the natural numbers. Let $c(j)$ be the coefficient of $q^j$ in the modular function
\[
J(q)=\sum_{j\geq -1}c(j)q^j=\frac1q+196884\,q+21493760\,q^2+864299970\,q^3+\cdots,
\]
so that $c(-1)=1$, $c(0)=0$ and $c(1)=196884$. Define the index set
\[
E=\{(\ell,j,k)\mid j\in\N,\ 1\le k\le c(j),\ 0\le \ell<j\}
\]
and, for $0\le\ell<j$, the number
\[
c_{\ell j}:=(-1)^{\ell+1}\binom{j-1}{\ell}(\ell+1)(j-\ell).
\]
Let $\cX$ be the set of symbols
\[
\cX=\{H_1(s),H_2(s),X_{-1}(u),Y_{-1}(u),X_{\ell,jk}(u),Y_{\ell,jk}(u)\mid s\in\C^\times,\ u\in\C,\ (\ell,j,k)\in E\},
\]
and let $F(\cX)$ be the free group on $\cX$. The symbols $X_{-1}(u)$, $Y_{-1}(u)$, $X_{\ell,jk}(u)$ and $Y_{\ell,jk}(u)$ are called the \emph{unipotent generators}. The commutator is $(a,b)=aba^{-1}b^{-1}$.

The set $\cR\subseteq F(\cX)$ consists of the following relations, for all $s,t\in\C^\times$, $u,v\in\C$ and $(\ell,j,k),(m,p,q)\in E$. The auxiliary elements are
\begin{align*}
\tag{Re:0}
\widetilde w_{-1}(s)&:=X_{-1}(s)\,Y_{-1}(-s^{-1})\,X_{-1}(s), & \widetilde w_{-1}&:=\widetilde w_{-1}(1),\\
\tag{Im:0}
\widetilde w_{\ell,jk}(s)&:=X_{\ell,jk}(s)\,Y_{\ell,jk}\Bigl(\frac{-s^{-1}}{c_{\ell j}}\Bigr)\,X_{\ell,jk}(s), & \widetilde w_{\ell,jk}&:=\widetilde w_{\ell,jk}(1).
\end{align*}
The relations are:
\begingroup\allowdisplaybreaks
\begin{align*}
\tag{H:1a} H_1(s)H_1(t)&=H_1(st),\\
\tag{H:1b} H_2(s)H_2(t)&=H_2(st),\\
\tag{H:2} H_1(s)H_2(t)&=H_2(t)H_1(s),\\
\tag{Re:1a} X_{-1}(u)X_{-1}(v)&=X_{-1}(u+v),\\
\tag{Re:1b} Y_{-1}(u)Y_{-1}(v)&=Y_{-1}(u+v),\\
\tag{Re:2} Y_{-1}(-t)X_{-1}(s)Y_{-1}(t)&=X_{-1}(-t^{-1})\,Y_{-1}(-t^2s)\,X_{-1}(t^{-1}),\\
\tag{Re:3} \widetilde w_{-1}(s)\,\widetilde w_{-1}&=H_1(-s)\,H_2(-s^{-1}),\\
\tag{Re:4a} \widetilde w_{-1}X_{-1}(u)\widetilde w_{-1}^{-1}&=Y_{-1}(-u),\\
\tag{Re:4b} \widetilde w_{-1}Y_{-1}(u)\widetilde w_{-1}^{-1}&=X_{-1}(-u),\\
\tag{Re:5a} \widetilde w_{-1}H_1(s)\widetilde w_{-1}^{-1}&=H_2(s),\\
\tag{Re:5b} \widetilde w_{-1}H_2(s)\widetilde w_{-1}^{-1}&=H_1(s),\\
\tag{Re:6a} H_1(s)X_{-1}(u)H_1(s)^{-1}&=X_{-1}(su),\\
\tag{Re:6b} H_2(s)X_{-1}(u)H_2(s)^{-1}&=X_{-1}(s^{-1}u),\\
\tag{Re:6c} H_1(s)Y_{-1}(u)H_1(s)^{-1}&=Y_{-1}(s^{-1}u),\\
\tag{Re:6d} H_2(s)Y_{-1}(u)H_2(s)^{-1}&=Y_{-1}(su),\\
\tag{Im:1a} X_{\ell,jk}(u)X_{\ell,jk}(v)&=X_{\ell,jk}(u+v),\\
\tag{Im:1b} Y_{\ell,jk}(u)Y_{\ell,jk}(v)&=Y_{\ell,jk}(u+v),\\
\tag{Im:2} Y_{\ell,jk}(-t)X_{\ell,jk}(s)Y_{\ell,jk}(t)&=X_{\ell,jk}\Bigl(\frac{-t^{-1}}{c_{\ell j}}\Bigr)Y_{\ell,jk}(-c_{\ell j}t^2s)X_{\ell,jk}\Bigl(\frac{t^{-1}}{c_{\ell j}}\Bigr),\\
\tag{Im:3} \widetilde w_{\ell,jk}\bigl(s^{(\ell+1)(j-\ell)}\bigr)\,\widetilde w_{\ell,jk}&=H_1\bigl((-s)^{j-\ell}\bigr)\,H_2\bigl((-s)^{\ell+1}\bigr),\\
\tag{Im:4a} \widetilde w_{\ell,jk}X_{\ell,jk}(u)\widetilde w_{\ell,jk}^{-1}&=Y_{\ell,jk}\Bigl(\frac{-u}{c_{\ell j}}\Bigr),\\
\tag{Im:4b} \widetilde w_{\ell,jk}Y_{\ell,jk}(u)\widetilde w_{\ell,jk}^{-1}&=X_{\ell,jk}(-c_{\ell j}u),\\
\tag{Im:5a} \widetilde w_{\ell,jk}H_1(s^{j-\ell})\widetilde w_{\ell,jk}^{-1}&=H_2(s^{-(\ell+1)}),\\
\tag{Im:5b} \widetilde w_{\ell,jk}H_2(s^{\ell+1})\widetilde w_{\ell,jk}^{-1}&=H_1(s^{-(j-\ell)}),\\
\tag{Im:6a} H_1(s)X_{\ell,jk}(u)H_1(s)^{-1}&=X_{\ell,jk}(s^{\ell+1}u),\\
\tag{Im:6b} H_2(s)X_{\ell,jk}(u)H_2(s)^{-1}&=X_{\ell,jk}(s^{j-\ell}u),\\
\tag{Im:6c} H_1(s)Y_{\ell,jk}(u)H_1(s)^{-1}&=Y_{\ell,jk}(s^{-(\ell+1)}u),\\
\tag{Im:6d} H_2(s)Y_{\ell,jk}(u)H_2(s)^{-1}&=Y_{\ell,jk}(s^{-(j-\ell)}u),\\
\tag{U:1a} (X_{-1}(u),X_{j-1,jk}(v))&=1,\\
\tag{U:1b} (Y_{-1}(u),Y_{j-1,jk}(v))&=1,\\
\tag{U:1c} (Y_{-1}(u),X_{0,jk}(v))&=1,\\
\tag{U:1d} (X_{-1}(u),Y_{0,jk}(v))&=1,\\
\tag{U:2} (X_{\ell,jk}(u),Y_{m,pq}(v))&=1\quad\text{if } j\ne p \text{ or } k\ne q \text{ or } |\ell-m|>1,\\
\tag{U:3a} (X_{1,2k}(u),Y_{0,2k}(v))&=X_{-1}(uv),\\
\tag{U:3b} (X_{0,2k}(u),Y_{1,2k}(v))&=Y_{-1}(uv),\\
\tag{U:4a} \widetilde w_{-1}X_{\ell,jk}(u)\widetilde w_{-1}^{-1}&=X_{j-1-\ell,jk}\bigl((-1)^{j-\ell-1}u\bigr),\\
\tag{U:4b} \widetilde w_{-1}Y_{\ell,jk}(u)\widetilde w_{-1}^{-1}&=Y_{j-1-\ell,jk}\bigl((-1)^{j-\ell-1}u\bigr).
\end{align*}
\endgroup
The group is $G(\mathfrak m)=\langle\cX\mid\cR\rangle=F(\cX)/N_\cR$, where $N_\cR$ is the normal closure of $\cR$.

The \emph{toral subgroup} $H$ is the subgroup of $G(\mathfrak m)$ generated by $\{H_1(s),H_2(s)\mid s\in\C^\times\}$. The \emph{unipotent subgroup} $U$ is the subgroup of $G(\mathfrak m)$ generated by all unipotent generators $X_{-1}(u)$, $Y_{-1}(u)$, $X_{\ell,jk}(u)$, $Y_{\ell,jk}(u)$, with $u\in\C$ and $(\ell,j,k)\in E$.

\begin{theorem}[Lemma 3.5 of the source]
For any $h\in H$ and $u\in U$ there exists an element $u'\in U$ such that $uh=hu'$.
\end{theorem}

The source proof cites Theorem 3.4, which states that $H$ normalizes $U$, that is, $huh^{-1}\in U$ for all $h\in H$ and $u\in U$. It sets $u'=h^{-1}uh$ and multiplies on the left by $h$.

\section{Formalization}

The formalization builds on a library of previously verified declarations. These come from the earlier formalizations of Theorems 3.3 and 3.4 of the source. The library defines $G(\mathfrak m)$ as a presented group in Mathlib, together with the subgroups $H$ and $U$. All library declarations live in the namespace \texttt{GM}.

\paragraph{The coefficients.}
Throughout, the coefficients $c(j)$ of $J$ are replaced by an arbitrary parameter $c:\N\to\N$. The proof uses $c$ only through the index set. The theorem verified here carries no hypothesis on $c$; in particular it does not need $c(2)\ge 1$. It therefore holds for the actual coefficients of $J$.

\paragraph{Index set.}
For $\ell,j,k\in\N$, $\mathrm{Valid}_c(\ell,j,k)$ is the proposition $\ell<j \wedge 1\le k\wedge k\le c(j)$. Thus $(\ell,j,k)\in E$ if and only if $\mathrm{Valid}_c(\ell,j,k)$ holds when $c$ is the coefficient function of $J$. The case $j=0$ is excluded in both settings: in the source because $c(0)=0$, and in the formalization because $\ell<j$.
\begin{leanblock}{Lean: \texttt{Valid} --- library, verified earlier}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},breaklines=true]
\PY{l+s+sd}{/\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ Index set E: (ℓ,j,k) with 0 ≤ ℓ \PYZlt{} j and 1 ≤ k ≤ c(j). }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}
\PY{k+kn}{def}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Valid}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{c}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℕ}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{→}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℕ}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{l}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{j}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{k}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℕ}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k+kt}{Prop}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{l}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZlt{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{j}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∧}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{≤}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{k}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∧}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{k}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{≤}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{c}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{j}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leanblock}

\paragraph{Generators.}
The type $\mathrm{Gen}_c$ has six kinds of elements:
\begin{itemize}
\item $H_1(s)$ and $H_2(s)$ for $s\in\C^\times$;
\item $X_{-1}(u)$ and $Y_{-1}(u)$ for $u\in\C$;
\item $X_{\ell,jk}(u)$ and $Y_{\ell,jk}(u)$ for $\ell,j,k\in\N$, a proof of $\mathrm{Valid}_c(\ell,j,k)$, and $u\in\C$.
\end{itemize}
Because proofs of a proposition are all equal, the last two kinds are indexed exactly by $E\times\C$. Hence $\mathrm{Gen}_c$ is the set $\cX$, and $F_c$ is the free group $F(\cX)$.

\paragraph{Constants and auxiliary words.}
The complex number $\mathrm{cc}(\ell,j)$ is
\[
\mathrm{cc}(\ell,j)=(-1)^{\ell+1}\binom{j\mathbin{\dot-}1}{\ell}(\ell+1)(j\mathbin{\dot-}\ell),
\]
where $\dot-$ is truncated subtraction on $\N$. When $\ell<j$ this equals $c_{\ell j}$, and it is then nonzero. As a result, the divisions by $c_{\ell j}$ in the relations below are genuine divisions, even though Lean sets $x/0=0$.
\begin{leanblock}{Lean: \texttt{cc} --- library, verified earlier}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},breaklines=true]
\PY{l+s+sd}{/\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ c\PYZus{}\PYZob{}ℓ j\PYZcb{} = (}\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{1)\PYZca{}(ℓ+1) * binom(j}\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{1,ℓ) * (ℓ+1) * (j}\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ℓ). }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}
\PY{k+kn}{noncomputable}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k+kn}{def}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{cc}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{l}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{j}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℕ}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℂ}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℂ}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZca{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{l}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{+}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{*}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{o}{(}\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{j}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{o}{)}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{choose}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{l}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℕ}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℂ}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{*}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{l}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℂ}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{+}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{*}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{j}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{l}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℕ}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℂ}\PY{o}{)}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leanblock}

\paragraph{Relations.}
The predicate $\mathrm{Rel}_c$ on $F_c$ has one constructor per tagged relation. The constructors \texttt{H1a}, \texttt{H1b}, \texttt{H2}, \texttt{Re1a}, \dots, \texttt{U4b} correspond to (H:1a), (H:1b), (H:2), (Re:1a), \dots, (U:4b). An equation $a=b$ is recorded as the relator $ab^{-1}$, and a relation $(a,b)=1$ as the relator $aba^{-1}b^{-1}$. The parameters range as in the source: $s,t\in\C^\times$, $u,v\in\C$, and index triples carry a validity proof.

Four points of encoding should be noted.
\begin{itemize}
\item In (U:1a) and (U:1b) the triple is $(j-1,j,k)$, and in (U:1c) and (U:1d) it is $(0,j,k)$.
\item In (U:2) the side condition is $j\ne p\vee k\ne q\vee |\ell-m|>1$, with $|\ell-m|$ computed in $\mathbb Z$.
\item In (U:3a) and (U:3b) both triples $(1,2,k)$ and $(0,2,k)$ must be valid.
\item In (U:4a) and (U:4b) the target triple $(j\mathbin{\dot-}1\mathbin{\dot-}\ell,j,k)$ is also required to be valid. This is automatic when $(\ell,j,k)\in E$. All truncated subtractions in exponents and indices are true differences whenever $\ell<j$.
\end{itemize}
Powers such as $s^{(\ell+1)(j-\ell)}$ and $(-s)^{j-\ell}$ in (Im:3), and inverses in (Im:5a) and (Im:5b), are taken in $\C^\times$. For example, $H_2(s^{-(\ell+1)})$ is written as $h_2((s^{\ell+1})^{-1})$.

\paragraph{Toral and unipotent subgroups.}
The set $\mathrm{torusGens}_c\subseteq\mathrm{Grp}_c$ consists of the images $\mathrm{mk}(h_1(s))$ and $\mathrm{mk}(h_2(s))$, $s\in\C^\times$. The subgroup $\mathrm{torusSub}_c$ it generates is $H$.

\paragraph{The library result used.}
The only library theorem used is \leanname{gsimple_torus_normalizes_unipotent}, the formal version of Theorem 3.4. It states: for every $c:\N\to\N$, every $h\in\mathrm{torusSub}_c$ and every $u\in\mathrm{unipSub}_c$, we have $huh^{-1}\in\mathrm{unipSub}_c$. It carries no hypothesis on $c$.

\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{closure\PYZus{}normalizes}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{GM}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{torusGens}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{c}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{GM}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{unipGens}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{c}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{torusGens\PYZus{}inv}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{c}\PY{o}{)}

\PY{+w}{      }\PY{o}{(}\PY{k}{fun}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{g}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hg}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{=\PYZgt{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Subgroup}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{subset\PYZus{}closure}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{torusGens\PYZus{}conj}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{c}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{t}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ht}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{g}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hg}\PY{o}{)}\PY{o}{)}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hh}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{u}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hu}

The library also contains the formalization of Theorem 3.3, \leanname{gsimple_g_m_perfect}, which states that $G(\mathfrak m)$ is perfect under the hypothesis $c(2)\ge1$. It is not used here.

\paragraph{The target statement.}
\begin{leanblock}{Lean: target statement \texttt{gsimple\_torus\_unipotent\_commute}, fixed before the proof; \texttt{solution} is checked to have exactly this type}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},breaklines=true]
\PY{k+kn}{theorem}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{gsimple\PYZus{}torus\PYZus{}unipotent\PYZus{}commute}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{c}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℕ}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{→}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℕ}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∀}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∈}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{GM}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{torusSub}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{c}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∀}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{u}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∈}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{GM}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{unipSub}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{c}\PY{o}{,}
\PY{+w}{      }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∃}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{u\PYZsq{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∈}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{GM}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{unipSub}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{c}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{u}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{*}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{*}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{u\PYZsq{}}\PY{+w}{ }
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leanblock}

In mathematical terms the statement reads: for every $c:\N\to\N$, every $h\in\mathrm{torusSub}_c$ and every $u\in\mathrm{unipSub}_c$, there exists $u'\in\mathrm{unipSub}_c$ with $uh=hu'$ in $\mathrm{Grp}_c$.

With $c$ taken to be the coefficient function of $J$, the objects $\mathrm{Grp}_c$, $\mathrm{torusSub}_c$ and $\mathrm{unipSub}_c$ are $G(\mathfrak m)$, $H$ and $U$. The statement is then exactly Lemma 3.5. Because $c$ is arbitrary, the formal statement is in fact more general.

\section{Proof}

\subsection{Overview}

The source proof has two steps:
\begin{enumerate}
\item By Theorem 3.4, $u':=h^{-1}uh$ lies in $U$.
\item Multiplying $h^{-1}uh=u'$ on the left by $h$ gives $uh=hu'$.
\end{enumerate}

The formal proof follows these steps exactly. Step~1 is the library theorem \leanname{gsimple_torus_normalizes_unipotent}, applied to $h^{-1}$ rather than $h$. This is legitimate because $H$ is a subgroup, so $h^{-1}\in H$; the source leaves this point implicit. Consequently the element produced is literally $u'=h^{-1}u(h^{-1})^{-1}$. Step~2 is the group identity of \cref{lem:conj_inv_rewrite}.

The proof contains no other deviation from the source.

For orientation, the library proves Theorem 3.4 in three stages:
\begin{enumerate}
\item Conjugating a unipotent generator by $H_1(s)$ or $H_2(s)$ gives a unipotent generator, by relations (Re:6a)--(Re:6d) and (Im:6a)--(Im:6d).
\item The generating set of $H$ is closed under inverses. One has $H_1(s)^{-1}=H_1(s^{-1})$ by (H:1a). One has $H_2(s)^{-1}=H_2(s^{-1})$ because $H_2(s)=\widetilde w_{-1}H_1(s)\widetilde w_{-1}^{-1}$ by (Re:5a).
\item An induction over the subgroup closures completes the argument.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{lemma}[\leanname{conj_inv_rewrite}]\label{lem:conj_inv_rewrite}
Let $G$ be any group and $h,u\in G$. Then
\[
uh=h\bigl(h^{-1}u(h^{-1})^{-1}\bigr).
\]
\end{lemma}
\begin{leanblock}{Lean: \texttt{conj\_inv\_rewrite} --- proved by AIProver, job 1, session 7}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},breaklines=true]
\PY{k+kn}{theorem}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{conj\PYZus{}inv\PYZus{}rewrite}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{G}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k+kt}{Type}\PY{n+nb+bp}{*}\PY{o}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{[}\PY{n}{Group}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{o}{]}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{u}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n}{u}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{*}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{*}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{h}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⁻¹}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{*}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{u}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{*}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⁻¹}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⁻¹}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{by}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n}{group}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leanblock}
\begin{proof}
Since $(h^{-1})^{-1}=h$, the right-hand side equals $hh^{-1}uh=uh$.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Proof of the theorem}
\begin{leanblock}{Lean: \texttt{solution} --- main proof from the captain's sketch}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},breaklines=true]
\PY{k+kn}{theorem}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{solution}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{c}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℕ}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{→}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℕ}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∀}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∈}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{GM}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{torusSub}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{c}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∀}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{u}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∈}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{GM}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{unipSub}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{c}\PY{o}{,}
\PY{+w}{      }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∃}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{u\PYZsq{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∈}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{GM}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{unipSub}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{c}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{u}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{*}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{*}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{u\PYZsq{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{by}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n}{intro}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hh}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{u}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hu}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n}{refine}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{h}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⁻¹}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{*}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{u}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{*}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⁻¹}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⁻¹}\PY{o}{,}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n}{gsimple\PYZus{}torus\PYZus{}normalizes\PYZus{}unipotent}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{c}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⁻¹}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{Subgroup}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{inv\PYZus{}mem}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZus{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hh}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{u}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hu}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{?}\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZus{}}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{conj\PYZus{}inv\PYZus{}rewrite}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{u}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leanblock}
\begin{proof}
Fix $c:\N\to\N$, $h\in\mathrm{torusSub}_c=H$ and $u\in\mathrm{unipSub}_c=U$.

Since $H$ is a subgroup, $h^{-1}\in H$. Apply \leanname{gsimple_torus_normalizes_unipotent} to $h^{-1}\in H$ and $u\in U$. This gives
\[
u':=h^{-1}u(h^{-1})^{-1}\in U.
\]

By \cref{lem:conj_inv_rewrite} in the group $\mathrm{Grp}_c$, we have $uh=hu'$. This is the required element $u'\in U$.
\end{proof}

\section{Independent review}

A referee model (claude-opus-5-5), which took no part in the formalization or the proof, compared the verified Lean file with the source. Verdict: \textbf{FAITHFUL}.

\paragraph{Definitions.} I compared each definition with the source.

\begin{itemize}
\item \leanname{GM.Valid} encodes the index set $E$: $\ell<j$ and $1\le k\le c(j)$. The coefficient function $c$ is a parameter $c:\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}$, which the problem-set note allows. The case $j=0$ is excluded automatically because $\ell<j$.
\item \leanname{GM.Gen} gives the generating symbols $H_1(s)$, $H_2(s)$ for $s\in\mathbb{C}^\times$, $X_{-1}(u)$, $Y_{-1}(u)$ for $u\in\mathbb{C}$, and $X_{\ell,jk}(u)$, $Y_{\ell,jk}(u)$ indexed by a proof of \leanname{GM.Valid}. By proof irrelevance there is exactly one symbol per element of $E$.
\item \leanname{GM.cc} matches $c_{\ell j}=(-1)^{\ell+1}\binom{j-1}{\ell}(\ell+1)(j-\ell)$. The natural-number subtraction $j-\ell$ is harmless because $\ell<j$.
\item \leanname{GM.wm} and \leanname{GM.ww} match (Re:0) and (Im:0).
\item \leanname{GM.cm} is the commutator $aba^{-1}b^{-1}$.
\item \leanname{GM.eqr} encodes an equation $a=b$ as the relator $ab^{-1}$.
\end{itemize}

I checked every constructor of \leanname{GM.Rel} against (H:1a)--(U:4b), including signs, inverses and exponents. They agree:
\begin{itemize}
\item (Im:3) uses $s^{(\ell+1)(j-\ell)}$, $(-s)^{j-\ell}$ and $(-s)^{\ell+1}$.
\item (Im:5a) and (Im:5b) use $H_2(s^{-(\ell+1)})$ and $H_1(s^{-(j-\ell)})$, written as inverses of powers.
\item (Im:2) uses $-t^{-1}/c_{\ell j}$, $-c_{\ell j}t^2s$ and $t^{-1}/c_{\ell j}$.
\item (U:2) uses the condition $j\ne p$ or $k\ne q$ or $|\ell-m|>1$.
\item (U:4a) and (U:4b) carry an extra hypothesis that $(j-1-\ell,j,k)$ is valid. This holds automatically whenever $(\ell,j,k)\in E$, so no relation is lost.
\end{itemize}

\leanname{GM.Grp} is \leanname{PresentedGroup} on these relators, which is $F(\mathcal{X})/N_{\mathcal{R}}$.

\leanname{GM.torusSub} is the subgroup generated by the images of $H_1(s)$ and $H_2(s)$. \leanname{GM.unipSub} is the subgroup generated by the images of all four kinds of unipotent generator. Both match $H$ and $U$. I found no discrepancy.

\paragraph{Statement.} The target says: for all $c$, all $h\in H$ and all $u\in U$, there exists $u'\in U$ with $uh=hu'$. This is exactly the lemma.

There is no hypothesis $c(2)\ge 1$. The source lemma does not need it, so the statement is, if anything, more general. Nothing has to be supplied to recover the source: instantiating $c$ with the coefficients of $J$ gives the source statement directly.

The quantifiers range over exactly the source's $H$ and $U$. The conclusion is not trivial by construction. Neither $H$ nor $U$ is defined degenerately, and $U$ is not defined to be the whole group.

\paragraph{Proof.} The file reproduces the earlier verified development of Theorem 3.4 as \leanname{gsimple_torus_normalizes_unipotent}, which states $huh^{-1}\in U$ for all $h\in H$ and $u\in U$. It is proved as follows:
\begin{itemize}
\item \leanname{h1_conj_unip} and \leanname{h2_conj_unip} handle conjugation of each unipotent generator by $H_1(s)$ and $H_2(s)$, using (Re:6a--d) and (Im:6a--d).
\item \leanname{torusGens_inv} shows that inverses of torus generators are again torus generators, using (H:1a) and (Re:5a).
\item Two general closure-induction lemmas, \leanname{conj_closure_of_gens} and \leanname{closure_normalizes}, extend this from generators to the generated subgroups.
\end{itemize}

The final theorem \leanname{solution} takes $u'=h^{-1}u(h^{-1})^{-1}$. It obtains $u'\in U$ by applying Theorem 3.4 to $h^{-1}\in H$, and checks $uh=hu'$ by the group identity in \leanname{conj_inv_rewrite}. This is exactly the source argument, $u'=h^{-1}uh$ and multiply on the left by $h$.

The Theorem 3.3 material (perfectness) in the file is not used by \leanname{solution}. It is unused context, not a gap.

\paragraph{Concerns.} None
